Prediction: This Will Be SpaceX's Stock Price by the End of 2030

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SpaceX is growing at a staggering pace, but does the stock price already reflect it?

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 19, 2026 at 3:00AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Space Exploration Technologies ›

If you had invested in Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.27%) shortly after its IPO and held on until now, you would have lost money. The stock debuted on public equity markets at $150, and shares are currently trading at $141 apiece, having risen as high as $225 and dropped to a low of $104. That's some volatility, and investors can expect more of the same from here on out.

But could SpaceX beat the market over the next four years despite the volatility? Let's see what its stock price might be by the end of 2030.

SpaceX made its name by revolutionizing the space launch industry, which has also allowed it to become the leader in satellite-based connectivity. And it has added artificial intelligence (AI) services to its portfolio. All three businesses are doing fairly well, but connectivity and Super Intelligence (SI) are growing much faster right now, and in the third quarter, both were profitable.

SpaceX generated $14.6 billion in revenue in the third quarter, growing by 161% year over year, and CEO Elon Musk said the top-line could grow by about 70% quarter-over-quarter in the fourth quarter. So we could be looking at fourth-quarter revenue of $24.8 billion, and total 2026 revenue of about $51.9 billion.

Here's the best part. Musk also said that SpaceX could generate $1 trillion in revenue by 2030, with a nonzero probability of achieving this milestone in 2029. If SpaceX can really reach $1 trillion in revenue by 2030, it could grow its top line at a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 109.6% over the next four years. That would be extraordinary. But how would that translate into profits? Note that SpaceX was profitable in the third quarter.

It generated a net income of $4.4 billion, up from the $1.1 billion net loss recorded in the prior-year quarter. It remains unprofitable over the trailing-12-month period, but at its current pace, it should be profitable next year. And things could get better from then on.

SpaceX's next-gen rocket, Starship, is designed to be fully reusable and could reduce the cost of reaching orbit by 99%, according to the company. Musk has said that once Starship is flying regularly, the company will wind down older rockets, Falcon 9 and Falcon Heavy. That could lead to lower expenses and higher profits within its space launch division.

SpaceX will likely continue to spend a significant amount on capex to tap into large AI opportunities while launching its next-gen Starlink V3 satellites, which will help it improve its connectivity business. These initiatives could pay for themselves several times over if successful.

So, in this scenario, SpaceX's earnings per share could grow substantially as well through 2030, perhaps at a CAGR of 50% -- or more. Under these assumptions, SpaceX's current forward price-to-earnings ratio of 49.03 doesn't look that unreasonable.

That said, this scenario looks a bit too optimistic, in my view. For one, Musk's long-term projections for his businesses often don't come to pass. That has been demonstrated time and time again with Tesla (TSLA +0.95%). Second, SpaceX faces considerable uncertainty across both its space launch and SI segments. Regulatory obstacles could weigh on the company, even if it performs well over the medium term. It could also fail to scale Starship launches as fast as it expects, among many other things that could go wrong. Third, competition could intensify over the next few years.

No company is likely to overtake SpaceX's lead in space launch or satellite-based connectivity, but increased competition could force the market to readjust its expectations. Here's one more potential issue: The AI industry may not grow as fast as the company expects. Putting all that together, it seems unlikely to me that SpaceX will generate $1 trillion in revenue by 2030 -- estimates between about $200 billion and $300 billion look much more reasonable, in my view.

Assume about $230 billion in revenue by 2030, and suppose that SpaceX's current price-to-sales ratio of 57.1 drops massively to about 12, as the market adjusts its expectations amid increasing competition and other factors. Then, SpaceX could have a market cap of $2.76 trillion by 2030, for an average annualized return of about 10%. Under these assumptions, the stock could be worth $203 by 2030. Those aren't bad returns, but remember that there is plenty of uncertainty ahead. Only investors comfortable with risk and significant volatility should consider the stock.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 794%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 203% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

GTA VI trendjacking was already everywhere, even before today's release of the most- anticipated video game in history (see our GTA VI review for our Digital Art Editor's experience playing it). A lot of the attempts to jump on the bandwagon have been lazy and half-hearted, with brands merely using AI image generators to give product shots a vaporwave makeover.

The optical chain Specsavers has turned out to be an unlikely exception. Its unusual GTA VI campaign might actually be the best brand collaboration of the year.

A post shared by Specsavers (@specsavers)

That's right, Specsavers has entered into its "biggest partnership of all time’. It's sponsoring GTA... just not that GTA.

The teaser post above might have raised eyebrows among gamers given that GTA VI developer and publisher Rockstar Games has very limited collaborations. There's it's deal with the Miami Heat (and with Oakley for the GTA VI Collector's Edition), but there are only parody fake brands in GTA VI itself.

But Specsavers isn't sponsoring the video game that everyone's talking about. It's hooked up with GTA Transport Ltd, a British road haulage company based in Leicester. Which sounds a bit like Leonida, right?

“It might not be the GTA partnership I originally had in mind but a deal’s a deal. I'm pleased to offer every driver at GTA Transport a free eye test,” the company's brand partnerships and sponsorships lead Neil Liddle said.

Bugz Singh from GTA Transport Ltd said: “We were surprised when Specsavers got in touch, but we’re delighted to be the GTA they’ve teamed up with. Our drivers spend all day on the road, so they’re looking forward to their eye tests.”

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

A post shared by Specsavers (@specsavers)

Specsavers' stunt rises above most of the GTA VI trendjacking that we've seen. It's funny, and it's a lot more creative and memorable than simply throwing Vice City hues onto brand imagery. Best of all, it shows that Specsavers really knows its brand and its public, maintaining consistency with the message it's been using for over 20 years.

Specsavers has been telling the same joke since 2002, and it still works. In fact, it's become even more effective. The line 'Should've gone to Specsavers', coined by then in-house creative director Graham Daldry, is now so ingrained in the public consciousness that it no longer needs to be appended onto the campaign. It's already implied.

Having already sold close to 16 million copies before today's release, GTA VI is a cultural phenomenon with a reach that extends well beyond that of most games. An AI-generated vaporwave graphic can't make an unrelated brand a part of that, but Specsavers has shown that on-brand creative thinking can forge a memorable connection to the entertainment event of the year.

CISAC’s International Standard Musical Work Codes System Hits 100 Million Songs Milestone

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The system, which assigned its first code to ABBA's "Dancing Queen" 30 years ago, has been instrumental in helping songwriters get paid.

CISAC (the International Confederation of Societies of Authors and Composers) says its International Standard Musical Work Codes system — the tracking mechanism it created to identify and track individual songs — has hit the 100 million songs milestone.

“Behind every code is a song, a story, and the people who created it, CISAC’s director of business and technology said in a statement. “CISAC’s priority is to strengthen the connection between accurate data and fair remuneration for the creators the system serves.”

The first work assigned an ISWC code, or T-000.000.001.0, was ABBA’s “Dancing Queen” 30 years ago.

The code is an essential ingredient in ensuring songwriters get paid, especially in today’s world, where multiple collection societies around the globe track activity for each song, many of which have multiple songwriters. Each code provides a unique, permanent reference for a musical work, connecting it to information about its authors and composers, the announcement says, adding it has become key in sharing musical works data across the global network of song collection management organizations, also known as performance rights organizations in the U.S. Today, more than 100 societies around the globe are using the new system.

CISAC adds that close to 30 publishers are allocating ISWCs by themselves and validating those ISWCs against the database. It says the major digital service providers (DSPs) and data companies have started to query the ISWC system, resulting in more than 20 million queries per month.

CISAC notes that it completed a “major modernization of the ISWC system in 2020… in order to improve speed, accuracy and efficiency of code assignment,” facilitating song activity and the subsequent responsibility of paying royalties to the rightful parties.

“Founded in Paris in 1926, [CISAC] continues to develop the international cooperation, standards and systems that help creators benefit from the use of their works as technology evolves,” added the organization in a statement.

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SHANGHAI, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- ATRenew Inc. ("ATRenew" or the "Company") (NYSE: RERE), a pioneer in technology-driven recycling and trade-in solutions for consumer products in China, today announced its unaudited financial results for the three months ended September 30, 2026.

Mr. Kerry Xuefeng Chen, Founder, Chairman, and Chief Executive Officer of ATRenew, commented, "We are pleased to announce that ATRenew has once again delivered solid growth in the third quarter of 2026, with total net revenues reaching RMB6,452.9 million, up 25.3% year-over-year. We have continued to refine our supply chain capabilities for pre-owned mobile phones and digital products, leveraging our industry-leading collection and fulfillment infrastructure to aggregate quality supply. By adhering to rigorous standards for refurbishment and preparation, we continue to advance quality standards in the pre-owned sector, offering users high-value, high-quality products. On the platform side, we have continued to execute our strategy of extending services to a broader base of small and medium-sized merchants, while enhancing the user experience and expanding the range of scenarios for our multi-category recycling business."

Mr. Rex Chen, Chief Financial Officer of ATRenew, added, "In the third quarter of 2026, ATRenew's profitability continued to improve, with income from operations rising 57.9% year-over-year to RMB190.7 million and adjusted income from operations (non-GAAP) rising 50.7% year-over-year to RMB211.5 million. We also saw a year-over-year improvement in fulfillment and sales and marketing spending efficiencyâa positive outcome driven by our focus on refined operations and economies of scale. Furthermore, we have actively pursued share repurchases, totaling approximately US$6.6 million in the third quarter, bringing the cumulative value of repurchases for the first nine months of 2026 to approximately US$13.5 million. Moving forward, we remain committed to scaling our business and enhancing operational efficiency, while continuing to create long-term value for both our users and our shareholders."

Total net revenues increased by 25.3% to RMB6,452.9 million (US$962.5 million) from RMB5,149.2 million in the same period of 2025.

Headquartered in Shanghai, ATRenew Inc. is a pioneer in technology-driven recycling and trade-in solutions for consumer products in China. Since inception in 2011, ATRenew has been on a mission to give a second life to all idle goods, reducing the environmental impact of pre-owned consumer products by facilitating recycling, trade-ins and distribution that prolong their lifecycle. The Company also uses certain non-GAAP financial measures in evaluating its business. For example, the Company uses adjusted income from operations, adjusted net income and adjusted net income per ordinary share as supplemental measures to review and assess its financial and operating performance. The presentation of these non-GAAP financial measures is not intended to be considered in isolation, or as a substitute for the financial information prepared and presented in accordance with U.S. GAAP. Adjusted income from operations is income from operations excluding the share-based compensation expenses and amortization of intangible assets resulting from assets and business acquisitions. Adjusted net income is net income excluding the share-based compensation expenses and amortization of intangible assets resulting from assets and business acquisitions and tax effects of amortization of intangible assets resulting from assets and business acquisitions. Adjusted net income per ordinary share is adjusted net income attributable to ordinary shareholders divided by weighted average number of shares used in calculating net income per ordinary share.

The Company presents non-GAAP financial measures because they are used by the Company's management to evaluate the Company's financial and operating performance and formulate business plans. The Company believes that adjusted income from operations and adjusted net income help identify underlying trends in the Company's business that could otherwise be distorted by the effect of certain expenses that are included in income from operations and net income. The Company also believes that the use of non-GAAP financial measures facilitates investors' assessment of the Company's operating performance. The Company believes that adjusted income from operations and adjusted net income provide useful information about the Company's operating results, enhance the overall understanding of the Company's past performance and future prospects and allow for greater visibility with respect to key metrics used by the Company's management in its financial and operational decision making.

The non-GAAP financial measures are not defined under U.S. GAAP and are not presented in accordance with U.S. GAAP. The non-GAAP financial measures have limitations as analytical tools. One of the key limitations of using non-GAAP financial measures is that they do not reflect all items of income and expense that affect the Company's operations. The share-based compensation expenses, amortization of intangible assets resulting from assets and business acquisitions and tax effects of amortization of intangible assets resulting from assets and business acquisitions have been and may continue to be incurred in the Company's business and are not reflected in the presentation of non-GAAP financial measures. Further, the non-GAAP measures may differ from the non-GAAP measures used by other companies, including peer companies, potentially limiting the comparability of their financial results to the Company's. In light of the foregoing limitations, the non-GAAP financial measures for the period should not be considered in isolation from or as an alternative to income from operations, net income, and net income attributable to ordinary shareholders per share, or other financial measures prepared in accordance with U.S. GAAP.

The Company compensates for these limitations by reconciling the non-GAAP financial measures to the nearest U.S. GAAP performance measures, which should be considered when evaluating the Company's performance. For reconciliations of these non-GAAP financial measures to the most directly comparable GAAP financial measures, please see the section of the accompanying tables titled, "Reconciliations of GAAP and Non-GAAP Results."

Africa Retakes Top Spot as Most Attacked Region, Led by Nigeria Surge

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Breaking cybersecurity news, news analysis, commentary, and other content from around the world, with an initial focus on the Middle East & Africa, the Asia Pacific, Europe, and Latin America.

Nigeria faced more than double the cyber threats of the previous year, with Angola and Kenya also in the top-5 most attacked countries globally.

Organizations in Africa faced an average of 3,834 cyberattacks per week in October, a jump of 38% from a year earlier, making the continent the most attacked region for the first time in more than a year.

Nigeria, Angola, and Kenya were among the top-5 most targeted countries, with Nigeria topping the list with nearly 6,700 attacks targeting organizations in the country every week; that marks a 140% increase over October 2025, according to data published by Check Point Research earlier this month. The nations are among those rapidly pushing technological transformation of their infrastructure, expanding connectivity, investing in digital services, and putting sensitive data in network-connected applications and storage.

The fast transformation is exposing more opportunities for attackers, says Shayimamba Conco, chief technology officer and security evangelist in the Office of the CTO at Check Point Software.

Related:Chinese AI Project Scouts Spies in Taiwan Chip Industry

"All this rapid digital expansion is now happening within the region, but then, at the same time, this [expansion] is outpacing improvements in security maturity," he says, adding that security improvements are uneven across the continent. "Yes, some areas — let's say for example, South Africa — it's adopting cybersecurity at a much quicker rate than the rest of the Africa region, but there will be those regions which are still lagging behind a bit."

The significant increase in average weekly attacks pushed African organizations past those in Latin America as the favored targets of cyberattackers. In the first quarter of 2026, Latin America averaged more than 3,000 attacks per week, surpassing the approximate 2,700 attacks targeting African organizations. In September, the two regions essentially tied in terms of attacks, with Africa once again becoming the most popular target in October.

Globally, organizations experienced an average of 2,550 weekly cyberattacks in October, down 9% from September but up a third from the previous year. While Africa encountered the most weekly attacks, both Latin America and Asia-Pacific regions saw a greater-than-average number — 3,483 and 3,436, respectively.

Globally, cyberattacks focused heavily on the education sector, with the average academic institution suffering more than 5,900 attacks per week, up a third from the same month in 2025. The education sector has topped the global list of popular targets every month, with organizations made attractive to attackers because their large data sets and reliance on legacy systems, says Conco.

Related:Malicious Linux Implants Mimic Asian Mail Security Products

"There is a lack of budget when it comes to, not just IT, but specifically cybersecurity," he says. "A junior IT engineer has to manage these vast environments, which are hard to lock down by nature, because you've got all these students who need access to all these applications and resources."

In Africa, however, cyberattackers most often targeted financial services, government agencies, and the energy sector, according to Check Point's data.

The countries are racing to improve their cybersecurity postures and address persistent threats. Nigeria's National Information Technology Development Agency (NITDA) continues to work on the details of the nation's cybersecurity and cloud-sovereignty policies. Last month, Operation Serengeti 3.0 — a joint task force between Interpol and 20 African countries — led to the arrest of more than 1,500 suspects and the recovery of $105 million in stolen funds. In August, Operation Jackal IV, targeted West African crime syndicates, leading to more than 200 arrests.

Check Point also flagged the use of AI in companies as a potential weak point, finding that about 2.9% of prompts to generative AI and large language models (LLMs) posed a high risk of leaking sensitive information. The risk is steadily increasing over time, from 2.3% in August to 2.5% in September.

Related:UAE, Saudi Arabia Face Onslaught of Increasingly Complex Cyberattacks

For privacy-breaking prompts, Latin American organizations shouldered the highest risk, with 3.8% of prompts posing leak risks.

Interpol has also observed that attackers are increasingly using AI in a way that has impacted African nations. In its African Cybercrime Assessment Report 2026, Interpol found that 55% of reported cybercrimes across Africa used AI to some degree.

"Cybercrime has emerged as one of the most significant criminal threats to the region," Neal Jetton, Director of Interpol’s Cybercrime unit said in a statement in August. "AI is automating every stage of a cyberattack from reconnaissance and phishing to extortion and evasion. However, we see that when countries work together, cybercriminal infrastructure can be identified, disrupted and dismantled."

Rob is an award-winning, veteran technology journalist of more than 30 years, reporting on global cybersecurity issues, the latest offensive and defensive technologies, malware incidents, cyber conflict, and AI's impact on software and cybersecurity.

A former research engineer, Rob has written for more than two dozen publications, including CNET News.com, Dark Reading, MIT's Technology Review, Popular Science, and Wired News. He has received five awards for journalism, including Best Deadline Journalism (Online) in 2003 for his coverage of the Blaster worm. Rob also analyzes data on various trends using Python and R for both his reporting and his clients. Recent reports include analyses of the shortage in cybersecurity workers, annual vulnerability trends, and annual threat reports.

Rob holds degrees from Cornell University in Electrical Engineering and Computer Science (double major).

LIVE – Bitcoin Price, Gold and Oil Fall: Should the US Repeat Its $2.2T 2020 Rescue?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Bitcoin price is getting hammered in the aftermath of the FOMC minutes. Gold isn’t doing much better; oil and stocks have joined the sell-off. The markets are already nervous about the Federal Reserve’s next decision, and yesterday’s FOMC minutes gave us another reason to worry: policymakers are still talking about raising interest rates.

Almost all officials saw further hikes as appropriate under their baseline outlook. Two even floated the possibility of a bigger increase if inflation refuses to cool.

🚨A liquidity black hole is DRAINING the markets. Bitcoin price during the SpaceX IPO: $63,000. Bitcoin price ahead of the Anthropic IPO: $63,000. Gold and silver have shed an estimated $1.4 TRILLION in value in just 24 hours. The S&P 500 has fallen -3.15% in three… pic.twitter.com/jI3dfnYqTE

This is a nasty combination for markets. Bitcoin fell around 3.2% over 24 hours, gold futures dropped more than 3%, and WTI crude lost around 3%. The dollar climbed to a 19-month high. Wall Street was mixed, though, with the Nasdaq finishing higher while the Dow and S&P 500 slipped.

Here’s the comparison. In March 2020, the Fed was slashing rates, buying bonds and throwing emergency liquidity at a financial system under immense pressure. Congress approved a $2.2 trillion rescue package to keep households and businesses afloat.

Today, we are watching markets fall while the Fed is possibly making money more expensive.

October’s FOMC minutes weren’t exactly what markets wanted to hear. The Fed held rates at 3.75%–4% during its October meeting, but almost all participants believed further increases would likely be appropriate. Two officials also said a hike larger than the usual 25 basis points could be considered if inflation data came in hotter than expected.

And there was more in those minutes than just interest rates. Officials raised concerns about hedge fund leverage and the growing debt tied to artificial intelligence investments. Some financing arrangements could become a problem if investors start questioning enormous AI spending, and if it will generate the returns companies expect.

FED MEETING MINUTES (11/18/26):1. Almost all Fed officials saw “further action” appropriate2. A couple Fed officials believe a hike of more than 25 basis points may be warranted in December3. Some officials favored a 25 basis point rate hike in October 4. Returning…

The risk is forced selling. Leveraged funds facing losses or investor withdrawals may have to dump assets quickly, potentially pushing prices down further.

Gold has its own problem. A stronger dollar and higher interest rates make it less attractive to investors who can earn a return elsewhere. Bitcoin price, meanwhile, remains vulnerable when traders cut exposure to speculative assets.

The Fed hasn’t declared a financial emergency. Its minutes actually describe the banking system as resilient. But officials are clearly paying attention to risks that could amplify a market shock.

March 2020 was brutal. COVID-19 lockdowns brought businesses to a standstill. Investors rushed for cash, stocks collapsed, and Bitcoin briefly slipped below $4,000 after sitting near $10,000 just weeks earlier.

The Federal Reserve didn’t wait around. It cut rates by 50 basis points on March 3, then delivered another 100-basis-point cut on March 15. The federal funds target range dropped to 0%–0.25%.

On March 23, the Fed announced unlimited purchases of Treasury securities and mortgage-backed securities, promising to buy whatever amounts were necessary to support market functioning. It also introduced emergency lending programmes to keep credit flowing.

President Donald Trump signed the $2.2 trillion CARES Act on March 27. The package included direct payments to eligible Americans, expanded unemployment benefits, and assistance for businesses struggling to survive lockdowns. The Paycheck Protection Program gave small businesses access to loans designed to help them keep employees on payroll.

The response was enormous, but the economy was facing an extraordinary shutdown. Millions of Americans had lost their jobs, businesses were closing, and normal economic activity had practically disappeared.

Markets eventually bounced back. The S&P 500 recovered its pre-pandemic record by August 2020. Bitcoin climbed above $12,000 during the same month.

The current US economy isn’t in the same position as it was during the pandemic. Inflation remains above the Fed’s 2% target, and policymakers are discussing rate increases rather than cuts.

Slashing rates to zero and flooding markets with liquidity might help asset prices. It could also make inflation worse. Another multitrillion-dollar spending package would face an entirely different argument in Congress, especially without a pandemic-style economic shutdown.

The Fed doesn’t have to choose between doing nothing and repeating the 2020 rescue. If banks struggle to fund themselves or credit markets start freezing, it can step in with emergency lending and liquidity operations. That’s a much narrower response than another round of unlimited bond purchases.

Fed’s concerns about leveraged hedge funds and AI financing could become more relevant if investors start selling indiscriminately. A market correction is one thing. Forced selling spreading through the financial system is another.

At the moment, there’s no evidence that we’re facing another March 2020. The banking system remains resilient, and falling Bitcoin prices alone won’t trigger a government bailout.

But the Fed has a problem that didn’t exist in quite the same way during the pandemic. It may need to protect financial stability without giving up its fight against inflation. In 2020, Washington opened the taps. This time, we wait and see, and Bitcoin price will survive.

Bitcoin climbed back above $63,500 after its second dip to $62,000 this week, while Anthropic priced its record $81.3 billion IPO at $83 a share.

Bitcoin (BTC) rebounded from $62,000 on Thursday as US stock futures rose after Anthropic priced its record $81.3 billion IPO.

BTC/USD fell to $62,000 shortly before Wednesday’s daily close, testing the level for the second time this week, TradingView data showed.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Anthropic priced 980 million shares at $83 each on Wednesday, above its initial range of $70 to $78. The $81.3 billion offering surpassed the $75 billion record SpaceX set in June. Anthropic shares are set to begin trading Thursday on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

Nasdaq 100 futures were up about 1.1% early Thursday. In Seoul, the Korea Composite Stock Price Index (KOSPI) briefly reached 5,100.

KOSPI one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

“All of us have a sincere conviction that the AI exponential will generate so much value that, in the long run, it will far outweigh any shorter-term economic sacrifices we make as part of our mission,” Anthropic wrote in its founders’ letter.

Bitcoin saw modest relief from sell-side pressure during the Asia trading session, returning to circle $63,500.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

Wednesday saw net outflows of $306.5 million from the US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs), the largest single-day net outflow since Nov. 6, according to Farside Investors.

US spot Bitcoin ETF netflows (screenshot). Source: Farside Investors

Older coins were also moving at a loss, according to an analysis by XWIN Japan published on CryptoQuant.

The analysis tracked Bitcoin that had stayed put for more than 155 days. Over the seven days through Tuesday, those coins moved at prices about 6% lower, on average, than when they last moved.

Bitcoin LTH-SOPR chart. Source: CryptoQuant

Moving Bitcoin between an investor’s own wallets also counts as a transfer, so some of those coins may never have been sold.

Short-term holders also moved coins at a loss. Their ratio fell to 0.955 on Monday, its lowest since February, Cointelegraph previously reported.

'You will regret it': Angry resident confronts Democratic mayor during chaotic council meeting

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Sacramento City Council meeting descended into chaos after a resident confronted Mayor Kevin McCarty, threatened his political career and attempted to publicly reveal his home address, prompting officials to cut her microphone and recess the meeting. (City of Sacramento via YouTube)

A Sacramento City Council meeting descended into chaos Tuesday after an angry resident threatened the Democratic mayor's political career and attempted to publicly reveal his home address, prompting officials to cut her microphone and temporarily recess the meeting.

Video from Tuesday's city council livestream reviewed by Fox News Digital showed a woman confronting Sacramento Mayor Kevin McCarty during the public comment portion of the meeting, warning that protesters would continue disrupting city business if officials failed to address their concerns.

"If you do not vote in our interest again, you will regret it," the woman said. "We protest. We stop things. We shut things down, Kevin. And careers."

The woman then suggested that McCarty's political career had run its course.

XRP Flashes Biggest Volatility Warning Among Major Cryptocurrencies

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

XRP stands out as the cryptocurrency with the most alarming volatility warning among major digital assets, according to recent Coinbase data. The options market indicates that traders are now preparing for price swings that could be nearly double XRP's historical seven-day price move.

XRP's seven-day options-implied price move stands at 1.94 times its historical median. This figure far exceeds similar metrics for major cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH) and Solana (SOL).

XRP exhibits the highest premium above its own historical volatility. Solana shows the lowest premium among these four cryptocurrencies, Coinbase remarked.

As the chart below indicates, the options-implied seven-day price move for XRP is around 9.6%, according to Glassnode and Coinbase data as of Nov. 17. This compares to its historical median absolute price move of about 4.9%.

Basically, this means that the options market is signaling a price swing nearly twice as large as XRP's typical seven-day price movement.

Interestingly, Solana's implied price move is also close to 9.5%, almost the same as XRP's. Yet its historical median weekly price move is much higher at about 6.5%.

The key here is that although both cryptocurrencies exhibit similarly large potential price moves, the magnitude of the expected volatility surprise is significantly greater for XRP relative to its usual trading range.

For comparison, Bitcoin shows an options-implied seven-day move of about 6%, versus its historical median of about 3.5%. For Ethereum, these numbers are about 8% and 5.1%, respectively.

Obviously, this latest figure doesn't necessarily imply that XRP is going to crash. It's a sign that options traders expect a big price move regardless of its direction.

Implied volatility is a forward-looking measure. It's based on options prices. The higher the implied volatility, the more traders pay for exposure to potential price movements or protection against them.

The chart from Coinbase is based on a one-standard-deviation estimate. It's derived by converting annualized at-the-money implied volatility into a seven-day timeframe.

For XRP, the roughly 9.6% implied move represents a statistical range. It's not a guaranteed price target. A one-standard-deviation range suggests about a 68% chance of the price staying within this range under typical modeling assumptions.

Thus, the latest figure of 1.94x implies that XRP's volatility premium remains high despite a slight decrease from the previous reading.

From XRP traders' perspective, this can be a very challenging market environment. For traders using leverage, there could be a high risk of liquidation due to potentially sharp price moves.

“Wicker Nipples” and All: ‘Wicker’ Cinematographer Celebrates Steamy Film’s Viral Success

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"Now I know what 'wicker nipples' is in Polish," Lol Crawley quipped at Camerimage about the film that stars Olivia Colman and Alexander Skarsgard.

Wicker‘s filmmaking team is relishing its viral moments and box office success — not to mention its horny memes.

During a screening of Wicker at ToruńCamerimage Film Festival in Poland on Thursday, cinematographer Lol Crawley introduced the movie and expressed appreciation for it having become a social media sensation. Olivia Colman stars in directors Eleanor Wilson and Alex Huston Fischer’s romantic fantasy as a lonely woman who commissions a basket weaver (Peter Dinklage) to create a husband (Alexander Skarsgard) for her. Related Stories Movies Indigenous Queer Drama 'Princess' Is Dedicated to "Everyone Fighting for Their Freedom, Their Identity" Movies 'Sentimental Value' Casting Guru Recalls Meta Stellan Skarsgard and Elle Fanning Experience

“It’s my fourth time back at the festival and having the honor of screening a film in competition, so thank you so much to Camerimage,” Crawley said from the stage about the festival that honors cinematography. “I really do hope you enjoy it as much as we enjoyed making it.”

Crawley went on to recount the movie’s unlikely path from the festival circuit to internet obsession: “The film premiered at Sundance in January of this year, and since the trailer came out and thanks to all the love on social media, it’s just kind of snowballed. It’s gone from its premiere to wicker nipples being available and cardboard cutouts of Alex [Skarsgard] in all his fine wicker glory.”

Wicker has managed to heat things up since Black Bear released it theatrically on Oct. 23. With the makings of a sleeper hit, the film has grossed $23.7 million domestically and an additional $13.4 million internationally through Tuesday. In his review for The Hollywood Reporter, critic Richard Lawson noted its “winning mix of irreverence and compassion.”

The cinematographer acknowledged that the project, notable for its comedic moments, has a bit of a different tone than his previous work. Crawley won Camerimage’s coveted Golden Frog in 2024 for lensing The Brutalist before going on to win the Academy Award for the film.

“It’s a fantastic dark fable — quite different material for me,” Crawley added of Wicker. “But very, very enjoyable experience and very, very proud to present the film.”

Later, after a festival representative had finished translating Crawley’s speech into Polish for the crowd, the Wicker cinematographer quipped, “Now I know what ‘wicker nipples’ is in Polish, which is a takeaway for me.”

Aave App taps $91B Tron liquidity as it opens Android early access

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Aave's new Tron USDT deposits feed an Arbitrum-based savings vault, with withdrawals subject to available liquidity.

Aave Labs added Tron USDT deposits to the Aave App on Nov. 17 as it opened early access on Android.

The new route lets eligible users fund the app’s savings vault with Tether’s stablecoin, and Aave lists Arbitrum as a withdrawal option.

Aave’s product page lists USDT, USDC and GHO alongside its Arbitrum withdrawal option. The rollout announced Tron as a funding network and left native Tron USDT payouts unconfirmed.

TRON DAO said eligible users in supported jurisdictions can make the deposits on both Android and iOS. New Android users can download the app and join a waitlist, or use a Ghost Pass to start sooner, according to Aave Labs’ launch announcement.

Under Aave’s app disclosures, a user sends a supported stablecoin from an external wallet to the address the app provides. Funds that reach the user’s self-custodial account enter the pooled Stable Vault under the user’s signed instructions.

The vault checks the deposit asset’s price, issues shares, and records the position in a unit nominally worth one US dollar.

The vault keeps its balance and withdrawal records on Arbitrum. Aave’s technical documentation identifies Ethereum mainnet as a chain where the vault can deploy funds to earn yield.

The pooled funds can move between approved strategies as deposits and withdrawals change the vault’s balances.

The withdrawal request burns vault shares and creates a dollar-denominated claim, which Aave calls an IOU. Under the vault’s redemption rules, users can convert principal into that claim.

Converting accrued interest requires the vault to have enough assets, above what it already owes, to cover the interest being claimed.

Settlement burns the IOU and pays out a supported token. The vault needs enough of that token on the payout chain, and a system-wide limit controls how much users can redeem over a given period.

The redemption policy can also apply a fee that varies by asset. For example, a withdrawal in USDC depends on how much USDC is available on the redemption network, even if the saver originally funded the account with USDT.

Aave logs the vault position and withdrawal claim on Arbitrum. Redeeming that claim adds a separate liquidity check.

A withdrawal request creates a claim. Settlement needs an eligible token and enough liquidity on the payout network.

Aave publicly lists Arbitrum for withdrawals. Live payout liquidity, route-specific fees and settlement timing are outside this diagram.

Diagram follows the documented app funding path and Stable Vault redemption sequence. Lines show process order and conditional settlement. They do not show the exact Tron bridge route, elapsed time or value moved.

Aave publicly lists Arbitrum for withdrawals. Live payout liquidity, route-specific fees and settlement timing are outside this diagram.

A token payout goes to a wallet address the user has pre-authorized with a one-time code. Eligible users can separately withdraw to a linked bank account through a separate service that converts stablecoins to conventional currency.

Aave Labs says users can request withdrawals at any time without a lockup. The app’s disclosures say market stress, a shortage of available liquidity or problems with a bridge can delay settlement or make it temporarily unavailable. The rollout gave no settlement timetable for Tron-funded withdrawals.

The Aave DAO earns a fee from the gap between what the vault earns and what it credits to savers, but third-party charges may also apply. Network or swap costs can arise separately if the withdrawal involves converting one stablecoin into another.

In its Nov. 13 report, Aave Labs said user balances across the app had passed $45 million before the Tron rollout. DefiLlama’s Nov. 18 snapshot valued USDT on Tron at $91.7 billion, capturing the USDT supply on the funding network at that valuation.

Aave Labs advertised up to 7.25% annual yield in its launch post, subject to variable rates and eligibility. Aave’s separate Balance Protection program offers discretionary coverage for eligible users, subject to program limits.

Paul McStay on refusing Celtic autobiography request and saying ‘polite no thanks’ to Rangers

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Paul McStay paradox has been a feature of Scottish football for many years. Although his name continues to be sung at nearly every Celtic game, the man himself has been one of the least visible former players of the modern era.

He was an elegant presence in the centre of the Parkhead side’s midfield for almost all the 1980s and a good chunk of the 1990s, albeit injury dogged him in the later stages of his magnificent one-club career. A clip of a podcast he recorded with former teammate Brian McClair recently caught the eye, as it also featured a remarkably well-preserved copy of an old Edinburgh-based music newspaper called Cut.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

An equally well preserved McStay, now 62, and McClair discussed featuring on the wrap around front cover of the February 1987 edition. Pat Nevin was not the only thinking man’s footballer at the time. Now based in Australia, which might account for McStay’s healthy radiance as well as the infrequent appearances, all of this must feel like another lifetime ago.

So why has it taken him so long to publish his memoirs? That is itself a long story and is one of the reasons why McStay, whose family has playing ties to Celtic dating back to the 1920s, is launching his long-awaited autobiography at the home of …….Queen’s Park Football Club.

It was originally meant to be launched at the slightly more appropriate Celtic Park last year, while on a trip home to raise the title flag before the opening league game against St Mirren. “It didn’t work out that way,” he explains. “Basically the club sent an agreement for them to sign off on the book.”

Celtic were unhappy with some parts, probably related to the vexed matter of his eventual exit in 1997 after 16 years of stellar, some might even say genius, service.

The upshot is McStay has published his own book on his own terms. “I’m doing it independently, doing it on my own,” he says. “And I have been quite honest. It’s my book, my story, my life. So I just said I couldn’t sign the agreement. I’ll do my own thing.”

If the club’s mantra is let the people sing then surely allowing former players, especially such loyal servants as McStay, to tell their own story should be taken as read. “I just thought: No, I can’t have that,” he says.

McStay was sent off just once (at Dens Park in a 1994 League Cup tie v Dundee) and was described by Alex Ferguson, after his testimonial against Manchester United in 1995, as the “ultimate professional”. He was also awarded an MBE by the Queen and picked it up from Her Majesty herself in 1997 at Holyrood Palace. Happily married to Ann Marie, with whom he has six children, he is one of the most uncontroversial players ever to play the game. He nevertheless makes for ravishingly good copy.

“I’ve just got to tell my story,” McStay says. “If people are not aware of that, that's why I'm happy to share it. I just felt it was time to put how I lived it and my perception about certain things that happened as well, and put it in black and white.”

One regrettable consequence of the delay in his story getting out there is that we have been denied knowledge of a transfer tale to end nearly all transfer tales. Never mind music magazines, McStay would have featured on the front page of every newspaper in the land if the then Celtic skipper had joined Rangers in 1992.

The Ibrox club were buying nearly everyone else, including Mo Johnston in 1989, during this era, so why not make a play for the best player at their biggest rivals? The religion thing had already been put to bed with Johnston…..ahem. Manager Walter Smith made the cheeky enquiry through a mutual contact.

“That was a question put to me in ’92, yes,” confirms McStay, who includes this startling snippet in Maestro: The Paul McStay Autobiography. “It was a polite ‘no thanks’. I think that’s the best way of putting it.”

He is sworn to secrecy on the identity of who was sent by Smith to sound him out. “I can't say,” he says. “But I know he's a friend of Walter. I never spoke to Walter about it.” His relationship with the Rangers legend dated back to their involvement in Scotland youth teams, Smith as the budding coach and McStay one of the country’s brightest young hopes. “When you talk about mentors and things like that, Walter was up there,” he says. “Andy Roxburgh was youth team manager in ’82 when we won the Euros and Walter was part of that. Since then, there’s always been a bond. He is a wonderful man and obviously, he did well at Rangers, which had an effect on me. But as a person, I got on really well with Walter.”

The phrase ‘had an effect on me’ is a wonderful way of saying McStay, towards the end of his Celtic career, seemed perpetually pitted in an almost one-man battle to stop Rangers winning nine-in-a-row. It was unfair to him, just as the financial imbalance - McStay has a few things to say about Rangers' spending power in his book - was unfair for the rest of Scottish football.

Aberdeen rather than Celtic came closest to stopping the Ibrox side’s march. McStay endured six trophyless years between 1989 and an emotional Scottish Cup win under Tommy Burns in 1995.

Not long afterwards, reeling from an X-ray report confirming the “virtual obliteration” of part of his ankle joint, McStay accepted that he had to consider retirement. But intense rehabilitation work saw him make another return, although he was clearly toiling. He finally called it a day at the end of the 1996-97 season. The then owner Fergus McCann, along with others, helped make this already difficult decision a horrendous experience. He was given the feeling that Celtic thought he was “bluffing” about the injury in a bid to negotiate a new deal with another club.

“The exit wasn't great,” he says, understating things hugely. "I never ever thought I'd leave Celtic in that way. There were a lot of good times along the way, as well as ups and downs…

"But to me, it was very important to get that message out about how it ended. Because even at that time, there was a bit of PR coming out about certain things. And it wasn't quite accurate. I don't know what the club will feel about that. But I've got to tell the story the way it happened. And that's it.”

Encouraged by Tommy Burns, he gained his coaching certificates and applied for the Under-16 Academy coach’s position at Celtic during Martin O’Neill’s first stint in charge. However, former teammate Jim McInally got the job instead. Australia beckoned in 2010.

“I do enjoy the element of being able to hide away, out of the road,” he says. It must have been comforting to know that across the other side of the world, every time Celtic played, his name was being sung in the chorus of the stirring “Willie Maley” song, which will be familiar to nearly everyone who watches football in Scotland: “And they gave us James McGrory and Paul McStay….” It’s a paean to storied past, one McStay is an integral part of.

“That started when I stopped playing,” he says. “James McStay was actually in the original song and then, through time, the younger generations changed it to Paul.” Every day’s a school day in the company of a Maestro.

Paul McStay was speaking at the launch of his autobiography Maestro. To purchase copies of the Celtic legend's book go to paulmcstay.com.

That has lifted some of the pressure off the ex-Scotland international's shoulders, and he's spent the international break bringing back former team-mates Carlo Nash and Youl Mawene to his backroom team.

Still bottom of the Championship though on single-figure points, PNE will likely find the trip to West Ham a tough one, despite their recent poor run of form which has seen three defeats and a draw in their last four - Scott Parker's first home match in charge though has seen the Supercomputer tip the Hammers to be resounding winners this weekend.

Forget Leo Scienza - Southampton can seal promotion with want-away star

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Southampton have fought their way back from their four-point deduction to thrust themselves into the promotion race in the first third of the campaign, but areas of improvement remain evident.

Southampton, after starting on -4 points, have battled their way up to 7th in the Championship heading into the international break, and though they would fall to defeat to Preston North End just before the interval, spirits remain high around St Mary's.

Although last season ended in disappointment for a myriad of reasons under Tonda Eckert, the German has retained the faith of the board, the players, and the majority of the fanbase, as there is optimism that this could be a special year for the club.

With one of the best squads on paper in the entire division, that hasn't always translated onto the pitch, as the Saints must demand more from one of their star performers from last campaign.

Sweeping up the Fans' Player and Players' Player of the Year awards last season for Southampton was Brazilian winger Leo Scienza, and given his excellent debut year in Hampshire, it was hardly a surprise.

The 28-year-old scored seven and assisted ten in 39 Championship games for the Saints last time around, including in the play-offs, following his move from German side Heidenheim for around £8 million.

Making the left-sided forward spot his own, Scienza dazzled Saints fans with his displays, and naturally, his performances reportedly attracted interest from numerous clubs across Europe in the summer, including Everton, Tottenham Hotspur, Aston Villa, AS Roma, and Atalanta.

However, he would remain at St Mary's, and with two goals and two assists in 13 appearances so far this season, he hasn't quite hit the heights hoped of him this time around; if he continues to underperform, Southampton should look elsewhere in their squad for production.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Samuel Edozie joined Southampton from Manchester City in the summer of 2022 for up to £10 million, but in the four-and-a-half years since then, things haven't exactly gone to plan for the winger.

The 23-year-old, before this campaign, had made 81 appearances across all competitions in four years contracted to the Saints, consistently struggling for minutes and spending a year out on loan in Belgium with Anderlecht.

However, with one goal and two assists in just over 300 minutes of football in 11 Championship appearances so far this season, Edozie has displayed his ability to have an effect on a game, as Eckert must simply consider utilising the Englishman more often.

Heading into the final stages of the summer window, Edozie openly voiced his desire to leave Southampton to pursue a different opportunity, taking to Instagram to share his wishes with supporters.

Southampton reportedly rejected bids from Austrian side RB Salzburg and Italian side Lecce worth around £4 million plus sell-on clauses earlier on in the window, and given his lack of minutes, it was unsurprising he attempted to force through a switch.

As it happened, Edozie remained on the South Coast, and has so far played a bit-part role in their rise up the division, but with a clear abundance of ability, he ought to be deployed more often.

Southampton run the risk of losing him for nothing next summer, as if his situation remains unchanged, he won't want to sign a new deal, and the Saints could well fall into the trap of making a severe financial error in years to come.

If a Stock Market Crash Is Coming, History Says This 1 Investing Move Has Never Once Failed

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The market is sounding the alarm, but history has good news for investors.

By Katie Brockman – Nov 19, 2026 at 2:30AM EST

10 stocks we like better than S&P 500 Index ›

After a year of record-breaking gains, the stock market is wobbling. The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.23%) and Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -1.11%) are each down around 3% from their October highs, while the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +0.42%) has fallen by nearly 9%.

Investors are also feeling uneasy about the future. The Fear and Greed Index -- which measures investor sentiment on a scale of 0 to 100 -- sits at just 24, as of this writing, edging into the "extreme fear" category.

The unfortunate news for investors is that multiple market metrics suggest more volatility could be looming. However, history says the right strategy can set your portfolio up for long-term success.

The S&P 500 Shiller Cyclically Adjusted Price-to-Earnings (CAPE) Ratio is one market metric that has been sounding the alarm over the last several months. This metric measures the S&P 500's long-term inflation-adjusted earnings to track the index's valuation over time.

A higher figure suggests the S&P 500 may be overvalued, and eventually, the market tends to correct itself with a pullback.

In 1929, for instance, this ratio spiked ahead of the Great Depression. It surged again in 1999, at the height of the dot-com bubble. Now, it's currently at its second-highest level in history.

The Shiller CAPE Ratio isn't the only metric warning about valuations, however, as the Buffett indicator is also in record-breaking territory.

This indicator measures the relationship between the total value of U.S. stocks and GDP, with a higher percentage indicating richer valuations. It was named after Warren Buffett, who popularized it during the dot-com bubble, noting that when this percentage nears 200%, investors are "playing with fire." As of this writing, it sits at around 227%.

Investing in quality stocks and holding them for the long term is the historically proven best way to protect your portfolio against market crashes or recessions.

Many stocks can thrive during strong economic times, but those with weak underlying business fundamentals may not survive a bear market. The dot-com bubble is perhaps the clearest recent example of this, as countless tech stocks ballooned to record valuations, only to crash just a few years later when the industry collapsed.

That said, many tech companies from that era not only survived the bubble burst, but went on to become industry leaders.

The stocks most likely to pull through are those with a strong foundation, such as a durable competitive advantage, healthy financials, and a sustainable business model. While they'll still likely experience short-term volatility, they're your best bet for earning positive long-term returns.

In the last two decades, we've experienced multiple historic downturns -- from the dot-com bubble to the Great Recession to the COVID-19 crash and the 2022 bear market. Since January 2000, however, the S&P 500 has earned total returns of nearly 724%.

This isn't just a recent trend, either. Since 1919, the S&P 500 has ended every single rolling 20-year period with positive total returns, according to analysis from Crestmont Research.

No matter how severe the short-term volatility may be, healthy stocks have always thrived over time. By loading up on these investments now, you'll be well-prepared for whatever may be coming.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 794%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 203% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Claude is cracking down on abusive users — and 1 in 3 Americans could be in for a surprise

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Anthropic is cracking down on people who abuse Claude. Here's what happens if you cross the line

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you've ever lost your patience with Claude and let a few choice words fly, Anthropic has a new rule you should know about. The company behind the popular AI chatbot is cracking down on users who repeatedly abuse its models, and persistent offenders could find their conversations cut short.

Anthropic's updated usage policy, which took effect November 12, prohibits sustained and needless abusive behavior toward Claude. The company says the rule targets extreme cases rather than everyday frustration or criticism, but it also gives Anthropic grounds to take action against users who cross the line.

The change comes as new research reveals just how often Americans lose their patience with AI. According to an analysis of more than 8 million chatbot messages, one in three American AI chatbot users has sworn at or insulted ChatGPT, Gemini or Claude.

And if you're hoping a few angry words will finally get an AI chatbot to follow your instructions, the research suggests you might be better off taking a different approach.

Anthropic's new policy explicitly prohibits "sustained and needless abusive or cruel behavior" toward its AI models.

That doesn't mean Claude will shut down your conversation the moment you swear. Anthropic says the restriction applies to repeated cruelty without a clear purpose, not ordinary frustration, disagreements, creative writing or legitimate AI testing.

The company has already given Claude the ability to end conversations with persistently abusive users on Claude.ai and Claude Code. Anthropic first introduced this capability in August 2025 as part of its research into AI welfare and says ending conversations will remain its primary response to this type of behavior.

Sign up to the Tom's AI Guide weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editors and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

If Claude does end a conversation because of user behavior, users won't get locked out immediately. In most cases, they can start a new chat, provide feedback or edit and retry previous messages.

Anthropic's broader usage policy also allows the company to issue warnings, limit or throttle access, suspend accounts or terminate access when it suspects policy violations. However, it has not said that occasional profanity will result in an account ban.

Obviously, chatbots aren't human, and whether AI systems can have experiences that deserve moral consideration remains an open debate. Anthropic acknowledges that uncertainty but is choosing to establish limits on how people treat its models.

The timing of Anthropic's policy change is particularly interesting given how frequently people apparently get angry with AI. AI visibility platform Searchable analyzed more than 8 million messages sent to ChatGPT, Gemini and Claude by an opt-in panel of American users between September 2025 and September 2026.

Its report found that 33% of users had sent at least one swear word, insult or slur to an AI chatbot during that period.

The biggest trigger wasn't particularly surprising. Chatbots failing to follow instructions accounted for 19% of derogatory messages, followed by incorrect answers at 18% and misunderstood requests at 15%.

Other frustrations included chatbots refusing requests, responding too slowly, producing unnecessarily long answers or repeating themselves.

The research also uncovered some geographic differences. Nevada was the state where users directed the most derogatory language toward chatbots, while New Jersey was the politest. Among major metropolitan areas, Las Vegas ranked highest for swearing and insults, while New York ranked lowest.

Men used roughly a third more derogatory language toward chatbots than women, while adults aged 35 to 44 were the most likely age group to use it, based on the rate of offensive words in their messages.

Arguably, the most useful finding has nothing to do with Anthropic's rules. Searchable compared conversations in which users complained about a chatbot's response, looking at whether the problem was resolved after complaints containing swearing or insults versus complaints expressed in plain language.

The chatbot's next response resolved just 24% of complaints containing abusive language, compared with 35% of complaints without it.

By the end of the conversation, 35% of complaints containing swearing or insults were still unresolved, compared with 24% of plain-language complaints.

People may be more likely to use abusive language when they're already dealing with a difficult task or a particularly frustrating conversation. The study was observational, and an AI model evaluated whether the complaints had been resolved.

Still, the findings suggest that telling a chatbot specifically what went wrong may be more productive than insulting it.

Chris Donnelly, Searchable's CEO and co-founder, said users have come to expect a great deal from increasingly capable chatbots, even though those systems still make mistakes.

"It's easy to assume a quick response is an accurate one, but that isn't always the case, and a lot of the frustration we see comes from expectations not being met," Donnelly said.

"Telling a chatbot exactly what went wrong gives it something to fix, while an insult gives it nothing to work with."

Anthropic isn't asking Claude users to be endlessly polite, and its new policy doesn't mean you need to say please and thank you every time you send a prompt.

The company is drawing a line between understandable frustration and repeated, unnecessary abuse. For most people, that distinction is unlikely to change how they use Claude.

But the Searchable findings offer another reason to think about how you respond when an AI chatbot gets something wrong.

Instead of telling Claude it's terrible at its job, try pointing out the specific error, restating your instructions or starting a new conversation when the chatbot keeps making the same mistake.

You don't have to worry about hurting an AI chatbot's feelings to recognize that a clearer correction is probably a better use of your time.

What makes 'Grand Theft Auto VI' more than just a game?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When "GTA VI" is released on November 19, millions of people worldwide will rush to their consoles to play it. How has this video game created such a cross-generational buzz?

"Oh, it's so on now!" is the tagline in the latest trailer for the video game "Grand Theft Auto VI," or "GTA VI" for short.

"GTA VI" is a game of superlatives that's been crashing through records. The hype around it is unparalleled. No game in history has clocked so many pre-orders. It's thought that around 10 million people have already purchased it. There's no denying it: "GTA VI" is a global cultural phenomenon.

A few days before its release on November 19, fans can already start downloading the game. The file is so large that downloading it can take several days, depending on the internet connection. Providers have already registered a significant spike in data traffic.

As soon as the game is unlocked, players can start speeding through the fictional US metropolis of Vice City in a stylish sports car with criminal couple Jason and Lucia.

To mark the launch of "Grand Theft Auto VI," its real-life inspiration — Miami — is getting a full-scale "Vice City" makeover. This promotional push includes the Miami Heat basketball team stepping onto the hardwood in custom "Vice City" jerseys for a home game, a local festival featuring artists from the game's official soundtrack, and roadside "Vice City" signage installed across the area.

Expectations for "GTA VI" are sky-high. Nearly 5,000 people have been working on the game for more than 10 years. Development costs are estimated at around $1 billion (€900 million).

"Based on those figures alone — as well as the series' extremely successful and popular track record — this can certainly be described as a truly exceptional release," says Rudolf Thomas Inderst, cultural studies scholar and professor of Game Design and Game Studies at Germany's Neu-Ulm University of Applied Sciences.

When such an effort is put into a product, it's only natural for people to expect great things, he adds. Inderst is expecting "GTA VI" to showcase the absolute best of what modern gaming can do across gameplay, storytelling, visuals and tech.

This video game series has been around for almost three decades, setting new standards through its different versions. A biting satire on US society and the American dream, "GTA" parodies politics, culture and the media.

The game is also renowned for its gameplay freedom and spectacularly staged chaos in a crazy world where players can do whatever they please. Shoot down helicopters with a bazooka, go to the hairdresser's, take a stroll on the beach, go to a nightclub, take part in car races and much more.

What makes it a standout among video games is how its NPCs — non-player characters, those not being controlled by a human player — are brought to life. They respond based on a player's interactions with them, displaying individualized behaviors and traits ranging from fear to hostility. "GTA VI" has an estimated 600,000 animations for the civilians in the fictional state of Leonida.

"GTA VI" is essentially a huge adult playground, striking a chord across several generations. Millennials grew up with "Grand Theft Auto III," "Grand Theft Auto: Vice City," "Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas," and "Grand Theft Auto IV," while Gen Z grew up with "Grand Theft Auto V." Generation Alpha might still be too young for the latest edition, but will likely find ways to play it anyway.

Social media has certainly played a part in the series' ongoing success, particularly "GTA V" and its multiplayer spin-off, "GTA Online." Numerous content creators stream gameplay sessions on Twitch or YouTube, and particularly funny or crazy gameplay moments end up on TikTok.

At the same time, a dedicated role-play community has grown around the game. Players gather inside the world of "GTA" to act out specific characters, such as taxi drivers, judges or small-time criminals. They invent realistic backstories for their personas, and many even use accents to stay in character.

The hype surrounding "GTA VI" is not only good news for the game itself but could also boost the entire games industry by drawing more people back to their consoles. This is because the boom triggered by the COVID-19 pandemic was followed by studio closures and redundancies. Worldwide, it's estimated that 50,000 people in the games industry have lost their jobs since 2022.

But while publisher Rockstar Games is well-known for the meticulous polish of its titles and attention to detail, the perfectionism so prized by fans also has its drawbacks, especially for those working on the game. Rockstar, like many other game developers, has frequently faced major criticism regarding its labor practices.

Former developers report extremely long working hours and a heavy workload that resulted in health issues. Trade unions accuse company managers of employee surveillance, resulting in a culture of workplace stress and low morale.

Coinciding with the launch of Rockstar's largest game to date, the developers behind it are pushing for trade union recognition. That move would give them a collective voice to address working hours, pay, pensions and parental leave. If successful, their campaign could set a major precedent for the wider video game industry, which continues to face challenges around fair labor practices.

This article was originally written in German.

Wilgot Raises $3 Million Seed Round to Improve Product Visibility in AI Shopping

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Wilgot has raised $3 million in Seed funding to help retailers make their products easier for AI shopping assistants to find, understand, and recommend. The Stockholm startup is building what it calls Agentic Product Optimization, or APO: software that turns customer knowledge into richer product information and continually adjusts that content as shopping behavior changes.

Industrifonden led the round, which Wilgot also describes as SEK 30 million. Participants included Luminar Ventures, Foundry Ventures, Ventures Together, and Inovexus. Angel investors included Voyado founders Johan Bäckarlin and Johan Hjelte, alongside Niclas Mollin, founder of inriver and Occtoo.

The company will use the capital to develop its platform and expand its commercial reach. Anders Nygren of Foundry Ventures and Andreas Söneby of Industrifonden will join its board.

A shopper asking an assistant for a gift for someone who enjoys hiking is describing an intended use, rather than naming a product. For a retailer to appear in a useful answer, its catalog needs to explain which items fit that situation and why. A photograph and a short marketing description may leave important questions unanswered.

Wilgot starts with information retailers already hold: search queries, customer reviews, support tickets, and returns. Those sources can reveal purchase motivations, recurring questions, and mismatches between what customers expect and what a product delivers. The platform turns those findings into product pages, feeds, FAQs, and structured data.

For example, repeated questions about the fit of a shoe could point to a gap in its sizing information. Making verified guidance explicit gives both a shopper and a search system more useful evidence. The value lies in extracting a relevant fact and attaching it to the right item, rather than simply producing a longer description.

Wilgot positions APO as an active improvement layer. Visibility-monitoring tools can show whether a brand appears in an assistant’s answer, while product information systems store and distribute catalog records. Wilgot’s focus is changing the information those systems and assistants ultimately work with.

The company’s website describes three stages: discovering demand signals, generating product content, and measuring the results. Content outputs include titles, descriptions, attributes, and FAQs, generated in the relevant language. Its optimization loop follows visibility, engagement, and conversion performance across Google and AI search, then uses those signals to revise content.

Wilgot says this approach is designed for catalogs with tens of thousands of products. The funding release says its platform uses reinforcement learning to improve product information from performance feedback and supports content in more than 100 languages.

In practical terms, the feedback loop gives the system a way to learn which changes are useful after content is published. The release does not disclose its reward design or model architecture, so those details remain outside the public description. The claimed learning takes place in Wilgot’s optimization process; it does not mean a retailer can directly retrain ChatGPT or determine what an independent assistant recommends.

Structured product information already has a concrete role in search. Google’s merchant listing documentation explains how Product and Offer markup can make details such as price, availability, shipping, and returns available for shopping experiences. That illustrates the importance of making product facts explicit, although eligibility for a search feature is different from a guaranteed recommendation.

The broader issue is familiar across product data preparation for AI shopping: fluent answers still depend on accurate underlying records. A content system must preserve the distinction between a verified specification, a customer’s opinion, and an inference about suitability. Otherwise, making an item more visible can also make an unsupported claim travel further.

Wilgot names Daniel Wellington, J.Lindeberg, Myrqvist, and Signify among its customers. According to the funding announcement, net sales increased 20% across Daniel Wellington product pages optimized by Wilgot, while 7% of Myrqvist customers now arrive through ChatGPT.

These figures describe different outcomes: one concerns sales on selected product pages, while the other concerns a customer acquisition channel. The release does not provide the measurement periods or experimental design. They offer early commercial signals, rather than a single benchmark that applies across retailers.

Founded in 2024, Wilgot brings together Erik Wikander, co-founder and CEO; Dr. Mhairi Dunion, co-founder and AI Scientist; and Oleg Danylenko, co-founder and CTO. Wikander previously served as chief marketing officer at Lendify. Dunion holds a PhD focused on deep reinforcement learning from the University of Edinburgh, while Danylenko’s background includes data science at Klarna, IKEA, and Teemyco.

“Brands are spending too much time measuring whether AI assistants mention them, and not enough time giving AI a reason to recommend their products,” Wikander said in the announcement.

The Seed round gives Wilgot resources to expand that proposition beyond its early customer base. Its opportunity is to connect the knowledge scattered across a retailer’s customer interactions with the product records that influence discovery. As shopping interfaces become more conversational, making that knowledge accurate, accessible, and useful becomes a commercial task in its own right.
