I've been testing the iPhone Duo for 2 weeks — here's what I like (and what I don't)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two weeks with two displays, and it’s mostly smooth sailing

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

You can't fairly judge a phone's true merits after a few hours, or even a few days. I’ve been using the iPhone Duo as my daily driver for the last fortnight, and now feel I’ve had time to take stock of what shines (and what doesn’t) when it comes to Apple’s first foldable phone.

Is this one of the best foldable phones you can buy? Unquestionably. But that doesn’t mean there aren’t some imperfections to be wary of. The good news for anyone on the fence about this $2,000 handset is that even after 14 days, the wow factor hasn’t worn off. Folding away what's essentially an iPad mini and tucking it into your pocket just doesn’t get old — even though I wish more of the apps were optimized for the 1:4:1 aspect ratio displays.

The iPhone Duo is a first-generation product, and it offers a different proposition from the tried-and-tested formula of the iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max. But even when you compare the Duo to Samsung’s eighth iteration of a foldable phone, it’s impressive just how much Apple has got right.

Speaking of the Galaxy Z Fold 8, I’ve seen a lot of reviewers — including my colleague Mark Spoonauer — wishing the Duo was closer to that phone’s 201g weight. When I first picked up the Duo at Apple HQ, I didn’t mind the 254g weight — and I still don’t. It’s concentrated when folded but balances out when the device is open. The iPhone Duo 2 will surely trim the fat a bit, but for me the extra heft isn’t a strike against the Duo yet.

Here are a few other things that I like best about the iPhone Duo

It’s obvious, I know — but being able to run two apps simultaneously on my iPhone has proved a huge productivity win time and time again. For example, we cover a lot of live events at Tom’s Guide, and the iPhone Duo lets me keep Slack open on one panel with a livestream broadcast locked to the other.

It also makes uploading images a cinch. Keep the Photos app open on the left with Google Drive on the right, and you can just drag and drop pictures into your desired location. It’s a huge time-saver that I’ve gotten very used to.

Sign up to the Tom's iGuide weekly newsletter with coverage on everything from product launches to software updates, this is your go-to source for the latest updates on all the best Apple content.

This Duo-exclusive feature hasn’t been getting as much appreciation as I’d have thought, considering we’re accelerating towards Thanksgiving and the holidays at an alarming rate. If, like me, you’ll be trying to get sugar-fueled children to sit still for Christmas photos, this camera trick will come in clutch.

Fire up the Duo’s camera and when the phone is unfolded the outer screen will play some clips of Snoopy to grab their attention while you snap the perfect portrait shot. The only downside I’ve noticed is that the kids won’t be looking exactly at the camera because the Duo’s 48MP Dual Fusion camera is positioned to the side. Still, it’s a pretty ingenious little feature that’ll earn its stripes on Thanksgiving weekend.

Every single workday for years, my phone has sat face up on the desk, occasionally illuminating with a notification or a call. Since I started using the iPhone Duo, I prop the little guy up in “Tent Mode”, where the outer display shows my calendar, a photo slideshow or the music I’m listening to. If needed, you can also stream content on the outer display.

For the most part, I use it to display multiple time zones at a glance. I’m based in London and routinely need to work with colleagues in New York and Los Angeles. It’s true you can achieve the same effect with a regular iPhone and a MagSafe stand, but if you’re hot desking or working from coffee shops, then being able to prop the iPhone Duo into StandBy mode all by itself is a big win.

As I said at the outset, not everything is perfect with this phone, and there are a couple of things that give me pause when I get asked if the Duo is worth the price of admission. I’ve already mentioned app incompatibility, so I won’t rehash that here, but here are the two big sticking points that I bump into again and again when using the iPhone as my daily driver.

Apple is reportedly actively working on Face ID for the iPhone Duo 2 (along with adding an Action Button) and it can’t come soon enough. I don’t have anything necessarily against Touch ID — except in this case because I’m left-handed. When I’m using my right hand, I can swipe up, thumb the power switch and unlock the Duo in a split second.

But the majority of the time, I instinctively pick up my phone with my left hand and curl my index finger around the back to hit the button that way. I’ve grown accustomed to it but it’s slower and more awkward than Face ID and feels like a step backwards in usability.

The latest development beta includes something of a workaround: users will be able to activate the front-facing camera to unlock the phone within a 45-minute window of using their passcode or Touch ID. It's still in the early stages, but it could become a huge accessibility boost for lefties like myself.

Smaller tablets make for excellent notebook replacements, and this was one of the things I was most looking forward to using the iPhone Duo for. Alas, the only Apple Pencil I have in my possession is the Apple Pencil 2nd Generation from an aging iPad Pro. Which means I need to buy the Apple Pencil (USB-C) in order to use with my Duo.

I’ll admit, this is a minor hang-up; I expect many people already own the $75 Apple Pencil (USB-C) and are happy to have it working on the Duo. I’m just not one of them and would have appreciated if the $2,000 iPhone Duo worked with serviceable Apple tech I already own, rather than having to spend even more money to unlock its full potential.

Still, the good news is the Apple Pencil (USB-C) is currently reduced in the Black Friday sales, so if — like me — you haven’t picked up one to work with your Duo yet, now is the time to do so.

If you're looking for a basic stylus for your iPad or iPhone Duo, look no further. Apple's cheapest Pencil doesn't have premium features like pressure sensitivity or wireless charging, but it does offer pixel-perfect precision and everything you need for writing and drawing.

A lapsed gamer, he fondly remembers the days when technical problems were solved by taking out the cartridge and blowing out the dust.

First of Its Kind: Markley's Lowell Data Center Earns Arboretum Certification

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Once a vacant dumping ground, the Lowell campus now holds about 1,500 trees and more than 400 plant species, projected to remove an estimated 3.8 million pounds of carbon dioxide from the air over the next 50 years

LOWELL, Mass., Nov. 18, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Markley's Lowell data center campus has been accredited as a Level I arboretum by the ArbNet Arboretum Accreditation Program, the world's only international accreditation program specific to arboreta. According to ArbNet, it is the first time a data center has earned the designation.

The recognition places a working digital-infrastructure campus â one that keeps hospitals, first responders, universities and more than 500 organizations online â alongside botanical gardens, public parks and college campuses. Locally, Markley joins UMass Lowell, which earned ArbNet accreditation in 2023.

"We're proud to be the first accredited arboretum on a data center campus," said Jeff Markley, founder and CEO of Markley Group. "The digital infrastructure our region depends on now shares its ground with a thriving tree canopy that helps clean the air and supports local biodiversity. ArbNet accreditation gives us an outside standard to hold ourselves to as we care for this land for decades to come."

Measurable climate benefitsPreliminary findings from a study by AECOM, a global infrastructure consulting firm, project that the campus trees will sequester approximately 3.8 million pounds â about 1,900 tons â of carbon dioxide over the next 50 years.

According to AECOM, that is equivalent to taking more than 400 gasoline-powered cars off the road for a year, or the energy use of 231 homes for a full year.

The canopy also strengthens urban tree cover within the Concord River Watershed, supports pollinators and wildlife, and gives employees, visitors, and neighbors a green space to enjoy.

Seven landscapes, one living collectionWorking with Stantec, a global design and engineering firm, Markley turned the site cleanup into a designed landscape of about 1,500 trees and more than 400 plant species. Stantec describes the project as starting as just a corporate campus landscape that has evolved into a curated collection of trees and diverse plant communities, chosen for form, texture and ecological value.  The campus access loop, once purely utilitarian, now leads visitors through a sequence of seven distinct outdoor rooms that change with every season.

The collection spans maples, deciduous ornamentals, Japanese cultivars, weeping specimens and evergreens. Together, the seven rooms show that meaningful habitat, urban forestry and critical technology infrastructure can share a single, seamless landscape.

What's nextThis spring, Markley, in partnership with AlvÃ©ole, will add beehives, wildflower meadows and milkweed to draw pollinators and monarch butterflies to the Lowell campus. The company also plans to extend the arboretum program to its other properties.

"Data centers power the digital economy, but they also sit on real land in real communities. With its investment in AlvÃ©ole's nature program, Markley Group is putting itself at the forefront of biodiversity data collection," said Alex McLean, Co-founder and CEO of AlvÃ©ole. "Nature is becoming something companies have to measure and report on, and the operators who act now and can prove it's working will set the standard for the industry."

The Jeff Markley Arboretum will open to the public each year to celebrate Arbor Day. ArbNet accreditation is valid for five years and can then be renewed.

For more information, visit  https://www.jeffmarkleyarboretum.com/.

About Markley LowellSince 2015, Markley's Lowell colocation center has provided the digital infrastructure behind Massachusetts' essential services. More than 500 clients across every major industry rely on Markley Lowell for the systems that support email, financial and medical records, pension and education records, government services and first responders â nearly every part of digital life. Markley Lowell is committed to investing in and strengthening the Lowell community.

Bernstein said Oracle stock is cheap even if its AI data center business vanishes

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Analyst Mark Moerdler estimated Oracle stock would be worth $133 in that scenario, compared with a $115 close on Tuesday

Bernstein analyst Mark Moerdler said Oracle $ORCL stock is priced below its value even in a downside case where its AI data center operations vanish but the debt taken on for them stays, according to MarketWatch.

Moerdler put the stock's value at $133 in that scenario. Oracle stock ended Tuesday's session at $115. It last closed higher than $133 in late October, according to MarketWatch.

"We believe this is an extremely unlikely scenario, but it is amongst the worst possible conditions, excluding an AI Winter where generative AI fails within the next few years globally," Moerdler wrote in a Tuesday note to clients, according to MarketWatch.

Moerdler said investors may have factored in a partial "AI winter," a situation where Oracle would be unable to sublease or find new uses for the leases it already holds. That would leave the company on the hook for roughly $288 billion of data center lease obligations. He also argued that cloud-services demand would help cover the costs of Oracle's expansion even if AI lost its appeal, given his forecast that the market could climb to as much as $1.4 trillion. Some of Oracle's data center leases run as long as 19 years, which caps how much the company must pay each year, he said.

Moerdler said many investors find Oracle's software business "opaque," and some contend it merits a lower valuation. He retained his outperform rating but cautioned that sentiment among investors may be slow to turn.

Oracle stock has lost roughly 40% of its value in 2026 and is headed for its weakest annual performance since 2001, according to MarketWatch. Shareholders have grown worried that Oracle may struggle to earn adequate returns and repay the debt it took on for its data center buildout. CreditSights analysts said earlier this month that investors price Oracle's debt as if it were high-yield, even though it still carries a low investment-grade rating.

Oracle filed to sell $10 billion of senior notes and $10 billion of common stock on Nov. 2 to fund its AI infrastructure buildout. Oracle stock fell more than 6% that day. The company carried $125.34 billion of debt on Aug. 31, most of it in senior notes. S&P Global $SPGI Ratings cut its rating to BBB-, one step short of high-yield territory, in July.

So you've played through the GTA 6 intro, gotten to know Jason and Lucia a bit, and probably been taught how to drive, shoot, and change clothes. You finally reach that moment in an open world game where, for the first time, you're let off the rails to just be.

That's what I want to know, because I suspect we all have a different system or detail that we've been thinking about since the first GTA 6 trailers and leaks—a test we must conduct to determine if "Rockstar really thought of that." I'm talking about hoops shooting, snack eating, or going on a classic GTA rampage.

Sound off in the comments below (there's a button at the top of the page to skip straight to them) to share your night one plans.

I'm gonna punch someone in the face. I swear I'm not usually one to play Rockstar games like a psychopath, but a bit of random crime will answer a question I've had about GTA 6 for months now: Is evading cops actually interesting? Gameplay leaks suggest that Rockstar has implemented a proper stealth suite with threat indicators and search zones. We also learned that law enforcement will be able to identify Jason and Lucia based on what they were wearing and driving while raising a ruckus. That sounds super interesting, and I can't wait to put those systems through their paces.

I'm going shopping! Actually, I'll probably need some cash first. I will do some crime, and then I will go on a lovely shopping spree. Dressing up my digital counterparts is the main reason I play videogames, mostly because it means that, at least for a few hours, I'm not spending real money on real trainers or the high-quality fabrics that I love to drape over myself.

Going for a drive. I'm also a sucker for playing Barbie with my GTA protagonists, but something I'm really looking forward to is jumping in a car and going for a long drive while cycling through Leonida's radio stations. Rockstar always has an excellent ear for music curation, and there's something about speeding down a highway on a rainy night that hits different. With GTA 6's map being Rockstar's biggest yet, there should be a whole lotta asphalt for me to explore.

Walking off into the woods. I shall find the first tree line and B-line towards it. What interests me more than the cars or even the crime is finding out what kind of wildlife will populate the stretches of land in Leonida. Ever since I heard about the wildlife logbook that Jason (and presumably Lucia) has access to I've been planning my nature excursion, someone's going to have to find all those 62 birds and see just how snappy these alligators are. Deadass, I'm going to be like that penguin from Werner Herzog's Encounters at the End of the World: The PCG team explained even if they caught me and brought me back to the city, I would immediately head back for the woods.

Refueling my car. I'm not exactly well known for playing GTA games in a *normal* way (if there is such a thing), like I spent most of GTA 5 riding around the backcountry on a dirtbike listening to the radio, and not much time at all actually committing crimes. While I am curious about Leonida's dirtbiking potential, my first port of call is the gas station. Having to refuel your car in a GTA game is such an alien concept I just want to see how the whole process works. How much does it cost? Is there an animation? Do I have to go into the gas station? I'll drive around a little bit first to empty the tank and then that's where I'm heading. I know, I know, I live for thrills.

MARA prices $185M debt offering after $1.1B Bitcoin sale

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Bitcoin miner is taking on new debt to finance its AI infrastructure expansion, with the Long Ridge acquisition adding nearly $69 million in annual interest costs.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings has priced a $185 million secured debt offering through its US subsidiary to help finance its $1.5 billion acquisition of Long Ridge Energy & Power.

According to a Tuesday filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission, the new notes carry an 8.75% interest rate and mature in 2032. They will be added to $600 million in existing Long Ridge notes carrying the same rate, bringing the combined principal to $785 million and annual interest obligations to around $68.7 million.

The offering is expected to close on Nov. 27, alongside MARA’s acquisition of Long Ridge, which operates a 485-megawatt natural gas power plant in Ohio. MARA announced the $1.5 billion acquisition on April 30 as part of its expansion into AI and high-performance computing infrastructure. The Long Ridge property includes land that MARA says could support more than 1 gigawatt of data center capacity, although the company has yet to announce a major AI tenant for the proposed development.

The financing follows MARA’s Nov. 9 disclosure that it sold 13,597 Bitcoin after quarter-end for approximately $1.1 billion. It used $750 million to repay Bitcoin (BTC)-backed loans, releasing 17,477 BTC previously pledged as collateral.

MARA has sold billions of dollars in Bitcoin this year to reduce its debt. In March, the company sold 15,133 BTC for approximately $1.1 billion, using the proceeds to help repurchase $1 billion in convertible notes at a roughly 9% discount, saving approximately $88 million before transaction costs.

In its Nov. 9 shareholder letter, MARA said it had reduced debt by approximately $1.4 billion, or 37%, since the beginning of the year. However, the Long Ridge acquisition will bring additional debt onto its consolidated balance sheet.

Related: Luxor raises $16M from CMT Digital amid AI-Bitcoin miner convergence

According to MARA’s April 30 acquisition announcement, Long Ridge generated approximately $144 million in annualized adjusted EBITDA, extrapolated from the second half of 2025. The roughly $68.7 million in annual interest payments on the secured notes would equal nearly half that figure.

MARA also faces potential debt repayments next year. According to its third-quarter 10-Q, holders of approximately $924 million in convertible notes have the right to demand repayment in 2027, potentially creating another cash requirement as the company finances its AI expansion.

MARA shares traded at $6.57 at 6:53 pm UTC on Wednesday, up 2.34% on the day, but down 46% over the past six months, according to Yahoo Finance data.

In October, Sony Pictures hired veteran YouTube executive Fede Goldenberg to lead the division and change its direction.

Screen Gems has cleaned house, laying off a majority of its staff as the Sony division enters a new era under incoming head, former YouTube executive Fede Goldenberg.The division, which specializing in genre movies such as Resident Evil and Underworld and comedies such as Easy A, has let go of senior vp Nathan Samdalh, vp Ellie Walker, and creative exec Peyton Collins, among others, sources tell The Hollywood Reporter.Goldenberg made the cuts and is expected to bring in a new team as he takes Screen Gems in a new direction. The exec was hired in early October and is bringing in a decidedly 2026 focus to the label.Under the new creative strategy, the film label is expanding its longstanding focus on traditional genre films to include theatrical projects from the next generation of so called “digital-native storytellers,” meaning filmmakers who are making names — and gaining followers — in the digital content space a la Curry Barker of Obsession fame and Backrooms filmmaker Kane Parsons.Ashley Brucks, who had been running the label as president since 2023, transitioned to a producing role.Samdahl is the former Paramount executive who was instrumental in making the horror hit Smile (and was infamously let go from the studio before the movie became a surprise hit). He also acted as a creative exec at Walter Hamada’s production banner 18hz Productions. He joined Screen Gems in October 2025.Walker was also a former Paramount exec. She joined the Sony division in 2024. Collins was hired in 2024 after working previously at Jeff Robinov’s Studio 8.

Protesters heckle Trump's ex-Border Patrol chief offstage after Dem governor condemns College Republican event

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Video shows protesters heckling former Border Patrol chief Gregory Bovino off the stage at a Rutgers College Republicans event.

Protesters heckled former Trump Border Patrol commander-at-large Gregory Bovino off stage during a Monday night speech at Rutgers University, hours after New Jersey Gov. Mikie Sherrill called the Rutgers College Republicans’ decision to host him "wrong" and urged the club to reconsider.

Bovino previously criticized Sherrill online, accusing her of prioritizing illegal immigrants over New Jersey residents. After returning to the stage, he called her "Sugar Pop Sherrill" and "Kim Jong Sherrill." Speaking to reporters afterward, he said she wanted to "trounce this freedom of speech," described the "Kim Jong Sherrill" label as "very fitting" and called the governor a "totalitarian." He also told Blaze News that Sherrill had timed her social media comments to "foment violence" against conservatives.

At Monday night’s Rutgers College Republicans event, protesters began shouting within minutes of Bovino taking the stage. Rutgers police escorted him offstage and ordered the hecklers to leave or face arrest, according to local reports. Bovino returned about ten minutes later to resume his remarks and answer questions. Hundreds of protesters also gathered outside.

30% OF COLLEGE STUDENTS SAY VIOLENCE ACCEPTABLE WAY TO STOP SPEAKERS ON CAMPUSES, SURVEY FINDS

Attendees at Greg Bovino's talk at Rutgers shout him down and heckle the former border patrol commander off stage. (FreedomNews.TV)

"What’s so crazy is that you all allowed a Nazi to be here," a protester yelled as the heckling began. Another shouted, "Your fascist death cult is failing!" Video from inside the event captured chants of "Get out of New Jersey!" and "Immigrants are welcome here!" Outside, hundreds of protesters carried images of Renee Good and Alex Pretti, who were shot and killed by federal agents in Minneapolis.

Before Bovino’s Monday appearance, Rutgers told Fox News Digital it was committed to protecting freedom of expression and maintaining campus security. The university also said hosting a student-organized event did not constitute an endorsement of the speaker or his views. Rutgers had not responded to Fox News Digital's follow-up request for comment about the disruption by publication.

Hours before Bovino’s speech, Sherrill said in a social media post that she was "deeply disappointed" by the Rutgers College Republicans’ invitation, adding, "Giving him a platform is the wrong decision."

Sherrill said that if the club went ahead with the event, she would ensure the state worked with Rutgers to protect "the safety of everyone and the rights enshrined in our Constitution," and she urged attendees to question Bovino about his record.

SHERRILL COMMITS $20.2M TO DEPORTATION DEFENSE WHILE CONDEMNING RUTGERS OVER BORDER PATROL SPEAKER

New Jersey Gov. Mikie Sherrill is seen. (Getty Images)

Fox News Digital contacted Sherrill's team for comment on the disruption at Bovino’s event but received no response before publication.

Bovino served as Trump's commander-at-large of the U.S. Border Patrol and oversaw immigration enforcement and deportation operations, including Operation Metro Surge in Minneapolis. He was removed from command in January following the shootings of Good and Pretti and retired from the Border Patrol in March.

A woman walks at the campus after Rutgers University President Robert Barchi held a town hall meeting to students and people in Newark, New Jersey, April 8, 2013. Rutgers University plans to commission an independent review of how the school handled allegations that its men's basketball coach physically and verbally abused his players, Rutgers officials said on Monday. REUTERS/Eduardo Munoz (UNITED STATES - Tags: SPORT BASKETBALL EDUCATION SOCIETY) (Reuters)

During the Rutgers College Republicans event, a student asked Bovino about the deaths of Good and Pretti. He replied, "Poor decisions come with consequences," then said the circumstances were "still being sorted out." Bovino called ICE agents the "real victims" and added, "I love them and salute them."

Asked after Monday’s event whether he stood by the deaths of Pretti and Good, Bovino said, "I didn’t stand by anybody’s death."

He clarified that his earlier comment about going "harder" referred to mass deportation operations, not the shootings of Good or Pretti. Bovino said he would rejoin the Border Patrol "in an instant" to continue those operations and called the end of enforcement activity in Minnesota "the great surrender." He added that he hoped the administration would complete its mass deportation efforts but feared it would "surrender" its aggressive deportation agenda.

'I just hope GTA 6 is the last of its kind': Warren Spector congratulates Rockstar on its landmark accomplishment but says the industry can't keep this up

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"I'd rather have hundreds of games and full employment than one game no matter how amazing it is."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Game industry legend Warren Spector retired in August, and somewhat ironically he's been more outspoken than ever about the state of the games industry. After blowing up over the closure of BioWare in October, he took a few weeks to think about it and got even more pissed off—and now he's shared some thoughts on the biggest videogame release of all time, Grand Theft Auto 6.

Unlike the BioWare mess, Spector's not angry about GTA 6, but he's clearly ambivalent: His latest missive on LinkedIn opens with a congratulations to the dev team and says, "It looks like it will be a high point of sorts for our medium and our business," which right out of the gate suggests that he's not entirely upbeat about it.

Spector wasted no time getting to the point. "Its billion-dollar development cost and an undoubtedly crazy marketing budget don't represent a business model anyone else can or probably should emulate," he wrote. "That's especially true when thousands of developers are out of work. A billion dollars pays for a lot of games and a lot of salaries.

"Personally I'd rather have hundreds of games and full employment than one game no matter how amazing it is."

At the same time, he said GTA 6 demonstrates the truth of a point he's made previously: "Give a game the time and money to prove its potential (key word, potential) and you have a chance of making something great. Underfund a game or force it out the door before it's ready guaranteed failure no matter how much potential it has."

It's not hard to see that as a reflection on Thick as Thieves, the stealth game released earlier this year by Otherside Entertainment, where Spector served as chief creative officer and creative director prior to his retirement. Initially announced as a PvPvE game, Thick As Thieves was reworked and scaled back prior to its early access release, and eventually just ran out of money, time, and steam.

But it also characterizes the state of the game industry as a whole, where a select crop of proven winners—like GTA 6—from a tiny number of studios are given whatever resources they need to flourish, and everyone else scrabbles for crumbs. Former BioWare stalwart Trick Weekes predicted in September that Electronic Arts would be reduced to "guns and football" following its acquisition by Saudi Arabia's PIF, and so far he doesn't appear to be wrong.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

In that light, Spector's ambivalence, and his conclusion, aren't hard to understand. "Again, congratulations. Ambition and innovation are necessary for the medium to reach its potential," he wrote. "I just hope GTA 6 is the last of its kind even if it's the best we have to offer."

Will ‘GTA 6’ Crash PlayStation and Xbox Tonight? Oddsmakers Are Betting On It

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PlayStation, Xbox and internet providers are bracing: 'Grand Theft Auto VI' might literally break the internet.

Will the recent outage apocalypse also strike the launch of Grand Theft Auto VI?

As of Wednesday morning, a major online betting site, BetOnline, says there’s an implied 83 percent chance of a PlayStation or Xbox outage after the highly anticipated game is launched at midnight.

Which makes sense. The last week has seen disruptions affect Microsoft, Apple, airlines, banks, the FAA and even the NSA. With everything going down lately, does anyone really think the biggest game launch in history will go smoothly? Related Stories Business 'Grand Theft Auto 6' Reviews: "I Never Want to Leave" Business 'GTA 6' vs. Hollywood: Industry Braces for "Biggest Entertainment Launch in History"

PlayStation and Xbox have been doing what they can to try and limit disruption. Both companies have urged players to pre-download the roughly 212GB game, which is the most pre-ordered title in PlayStation Store and Microsoft Store history (“Given this unprecedented demand, we’ve joined with Rockstar Games to allow players to start pre-loading the game nine days early, so they’re ready to play on launch day,” PlayStation said in a statement).

Once the single-player game is downloaded, a player should be able to play offline without making further demands on the console companies’ servers.

But this assumes the vast majority of players have planned in advance and don’t wait until the day of release to get the game — and how likely does that sound? While hardcore gamers have likely been locked and loaded for days, GTA 6 will almost certainly draw legions of casual players reading the game’s positive reviews and media hype. Plus activities like players activating the game, signing into PlayStation accounts, and playing via cloud streaming could cause disruption.

Other potential choke points are internet service providers. Comcast says GTA 6‘s first 48 hours of pre-downloads constituted the largest single event in its network history. The company further predicted that Nov. 19 will its busiest internet traffic day ever. “We’re about to break all-time highs,” Comcast chief network officer Elad Nafshi told Inc.

Surprisingly, for those hoping to play the game who haven’t yet upgraded to a PlayStation 5 or Xbox Series X|S, you’re in luck. The consoles are currently listed as in stock at major U.S. retailers like Target, Walmart and Best Buy. This might not last, however. The Game Business previously reported that at least one major retailer is expecting the consoles to sell out ahead of the holidays. “We’ve been informed that because of the on-going issues around hardware component availability, we won’t be getting the units we want ahead of [GTA 6],” the source said. “Demand will likely outstrip supply during the year end period”). So if you want to return to Vice City, you better buy a console before you have to steal it.

Trouble stealing a car? There's an app for that.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Vice City's glow-up in GTA 6 ushers in a new era of smart car tech, bringing keyless ignition to the series. That's great for car owners, but a pain for our criminal duo since you can't hotwire a keyless car. Luckily, that doesn't mean you can't steal a car with high-tech security. Modern problems call for modern crimes, and that's where the key cloner comes in.

Standard trackers on stolen cars aren't much of a problem—they just forbid you from making modifications at a mod shop unless you register the car as your own.

Advanced trackers, however, are a whole other beast. They relay your exact location to the cops, but you can outrun the signal if you're fast enough. Keep your foot on the gas, and the cops won't be able to track you until you slow down. Apparently, the solution to beat high-tech surveillance is still a good ol' fashioned pedal-to-the-metal joyride.

Trump administration rules out cyberattack behind week of tech outages

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Vice President JD Vance canceled his DealBook Summit appearance to attend meetings on the outages, which hit banks, airlines and agencies

The Trump administration said on Wednesday it sees no indication of a coordinated cyberattack, state-sponsored activity or any other malign actor behind the recent tech outages.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton and Secretary of State Marco Rubio, who also serves as national security adviser, described the incidents as separate events. They said companies patching vulnerabilities and upgrading outdated systems caused them, according to the White House.

"This is not a cyberattack, and this is not a coordinated effort by a nation state," Clayton said. Rubio said officials have "no indication, no evidence, and no reason to believe at all that any of these incidents are related to each other." He said the Microsoft $MSFT incident has nothing to do with the FAA incident, and the FAA incident has nothing to do with the banking problems of a few days ago.

Clayton said institutions are taking systems down to install patches after vulnerabilities were identified. He said some handoffs between systems are not coming back as expected. "This is just an upgrade in patching, an upgrade in systems," he said. Rubio said, "We are not under systemic attack."

Vice President JD Vance backed out of his slot at The New York Times DealBook Summit on Wednesday so he could sit in on meetings about the outages, according to CNBC.

CNBC said a senior administration official spoke with reporters during a phone call and asked not to be named. The official said the government has "a great deal of confidence" that the incidents stem from system upgrades, patching and hardware changes, or other "disparate" events. Asked how much artificial intelligence played a role, the official said it is "challenging to answer that question across all of these incidents," according to CNBC.

The disruptions in recent days range from a nationwide ground stop and a prolonged Outlook failure at Microsoft to a reported blackout on the classified communications network at the National Security Agency, according to CNBC. The outlet also listed software troubles at JPMorgan $JPM Chase, flight delays tied to a United Airlines outage, and glitches with Apple $AAPL products.

Clayton told CNBC on Tuesday that no cyberattack lay behind the outages. He offered the House Homeland Security Committee the same message on Wednesday, according to CNBC.

Sen. Mark Warner, a Virginia Democrat and the vice chairman of the Senate Intelligence Committee, told CNBC he wants “better coordination” on AI safety. “I understand why people are freaked out,” Warner said.

The Federal Aviation Administration attributed last week's nationwide ground stop to legacy equipment that became unstable when data flooded its network. The agency said its AI-powered SMART system was not connected to the disruption.

Microsoft said it had no indication that malicious activity caused its global disruption of Microsoft Outlook, Teams and Copilot. The company said adjustments it rolled out to boost serviceability were "not taking as expected."

Il Milan sarà uno dei club più attivi sul mercato invernale. Gerry Cardinale, patron rossonero, ha promesso nuovi investimenti per rendere ancora più competitiva la rosa milanista. Microsoft Rolls Out Its Biggest Bidding Upgrade In A Decade via @sejournal, @brookeosmundson

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Microsoft Advertising is rolling out rebuilt automated bidding models. Learn what is changing for Target CPA and Target ROAS campaigns and what to review now.

Microsoft Advertising is rolling out what it calls its biggest bidding model upgrade in a decade. The new models use more signals to predict whether an ad click will turn into a conversion and what it might be worth.

Microsoft will apply the upgrade automatically as it reaches more accounts. That means campaigns can start spending differently before you make any changes yourself. Microsoft announced the rollout on November 18 and plans more bidding updates in 2027.

Microsoft says the new models can change how quickly campaigns spend and how closely they stick to your targets. These are the areas to keep an eye on:

A $50 CPA target still leaves room for a campaign to bring in conversions at $40 each, even if it spends its full budget. A max CPC can limit that flexibility. Say you average $3 a click and cap bids at $5. The cap rules out a $6 bid even if Microsoft expects the click could bring in a conversion within your CPA target.

Say you raise your CPA target on Monday and customers usually take a week to buy. By Friday, you know what Monday’s clicks cost, but you may still be waiting on some of the sales. Faster bidding won’t shorten the time those customers take to buy.

Raising a CPA target or lowering a ROAS target can open up auctions your campaign previously passed up. To limit swings in performance, Microsoft suggests making those changes in 15% increments and reviewing each after one conversion cycle. For a bigger change, like switching bid strategies or adding a conversion goal, Microsoft recommends allowing one to two cycles.

Microsoft recommends conversion-based bidding for new campaigns once the account has at least five conversions. Those can come from campaigns you already run. If you’re still gathering your first five, Enhanced CPC is an option.

On LinkedIn, Sophie Westall asked if the same advice applies to low-volume accounts already using Enhanced CPC. Microsoft Advertising Liaison Navah Hopkins replied:

Every account is different so a blanket recommendation still needs to be put to the test. With that said, so long as the account has at least 5 conversions total, the system is smart enough to use that to begin with conversion-based bidding. While we still recommend the 30-day evaluation period and not making too many changes if you’re working with fewer conversions, the system is much more responsive now!

For judging performance, Microsoft recommends around 30 conversions in 30 days at the campaign or portfolio level. A low-volume campaign can reach the end of that first month and still have too few conversions to judge the switch.

Microsoft reported 10% more conversion value per click at roughly the same ROAS after the model upgrade. That result came from August 2026 global Search and Audience Network data for advertisers using Maximize Conversion Value or Target ROAS.

Microsoft also looked at advertisers who switched bid strategies. Search advertisers who moved from Target CPA to Target ROAS saw 8% more conversion value at a similar ROAS. Advertisers who moved from Enhanced CPC to Maximize Conversions with a CPA target saw 18% more conversions at a similar CPA.

If you’re considering Target ROAS, pull up a few recent purchases in Microsoft Advertising and compare the reported values with the order totals. A $40 order and a $400 order can both show up as conversions even if your tracking assigns them the same value. That’s easy to miss if you’re used to checking that purchases are coming through and leaving it there.

Microsoft reported its largest gains from value-based bidding among advertisers who sent actual transaction values. If your purchase goal uses a fixed value, the reported ROAS reflects that estimate. It can move as you sell more or spend less, but it won’t capture a change in how much customers spend per order.

With lead gen, you may need time to get a better read on what a conversion is worth. Two people can submit the same form, and sales may find out a week later that only one is a fit.

Pedro Bailaderia asked Navah Hopkins about sending an estimate at submission and updating it once more information comes into the CRM. Hopkins supported using the Conversions API or offline imports. She also advised setting a ROAS target that reflects the values you send.

If your team is still working out how to value a qualified lead, that’s worth settling before you set a ROAS target. A campaign can hit the target you set while sales is still waiting for more leads it can work with.

If a campaign already brings in the sales or leads you need at a cost you’re happy with, you can give the upgrade some room before changing anything. Keeping your settings steady makes it easier to see how the new models work with the goals and budget you already have.

The rollout may be more useful if you’ve been adjusting targets to get a campaign to spend more evenly. If delivery settles down on its own, you may have fewer reasons to touch those targets during the week. That would leave you freer to change them when the business needs more volume or can afford to pay more for a conversion.

Our favorite accessories to level up your gaming setup ahead of GTA VI

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Looking for a headset or a pro-grade controller for your return to Vice City? We have some recs.

Looking for a headset or a pro-grade controller for your return to Vice City? We have some recs.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If you buy something from a link, The Verge may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

With Grand Theft Auto VI set to finally launch tomorrow, November 19th, our long-awaited return to Vice City is nearly here. But after all this time, some of the gear you’ve used in San Andreas might feel out of place on the glamorous streets of Leonidas County. Luckily for you, we’ve gathered a few of our favorite Xbox and PlayStation 5 accessories to help your long gaming sessions run as smoothly as possible.

The 8BitDo Ultimate 3E — an upgraded pick for Xbox Series X and Series S consoles — is a very capable, comfortable controller with elite-level features, including a high polling rate and adjustable triggers. It’s on the expensive side at $149.99, but the customization and clean execution justify the cost. Read our review.

PlayStation 5 owners looking for a controller upgrade, meanwhile, might consider the DualSense Edge, Sony’s first-party pro-style controller. In addition to a variety of extra function buttons and paddles, the wireless gamepad sports adjustable trigger heights and swappable joysticks (in case the potentiometers start to drift). It’s a little bit heavier than Sony’s regular DualSense, and the battery life isn’t quite as good, but it offers customization you won’t find on the console’s default controller. Read our review.

Sony’s pro-style controller, the DualSense Edge, might suffer from shorter battery life than the standard DualSense model, but it features great software integration with the PlayStation 5 and plenty of customizable components.

Nothing’s worse than having to pause a heist so you can go hunt down a fresh set of AA batteries. The PowerA Duo Charging Station includes a rechargeable battery and replacement rear doors for two Xbox Series X / S or Xbox One controllers. Simply set them on the dock, and they’ll start charging.

PowerA’s PlayStation equivalent charges two DualSense Wireless Controllers simultaneously, with an included AC adapter, and comes with a two-year warranty.

The 2026 Roku Pro Series is the brand’s first OLED, and the 55-inch version retails for $999.99, which is on the low end of the price spectrum for OLED. Games feel richer and more immersive on OLED panels, thanks to their deeper black levels, which are the result of unlit pixels or sections of the screen. Each Pro Series panel also sports a 120Hz refresh rate, four HDMI 2.1 ports, and both low latency and variable refresh rates for gaming.

For a more premium offering, the Sony Bravia 8 II is one of our favorite screens for movies, with an engaging, immersive picture and excellent image processing. It has great gaming features, too, like a 120Hz refresh rate with variable refresh and an automatic low-latency mode. It’s not cheap, but the OLED makes for an excellent upgrade to your setup. Read our review.

Sony’s latest first-party headset, the $99 PlayStation Pulse Wireless, launches the same day as GTA VI. My colleague Cameron Faulkner recently went hands-on with the wireless headset ahead of its launch on November 19th, and came away impressed with its overall audio quality and comfort. The newest Pulse is also straightforward to set up and use, especially when paired with devices like the PlayStation Portal that support PlayStation Link, Sony’s proprietary low-latency audio connection. It also offers Bluetooth for use with other devices.

For Xbox gamers, it’s hard to beat the SteelSeries Arctis Nova 3X, which retails in multiple colors for $109.99 or less. The wireless headset is lightweight, comfortable, and compatible with a wide variety of other consoles and mobile devices as well, for those rare moments when you aren’t playing GTA VI.

If you’d rather the simplicity of a wired option, Sony’s InZone H6 Air skips the batteries and dongles, instead opting to plug straight into your controller. This open-back gaming headset is lightweight and comfortable, which is great for long hours and late nights. My colleague Cameron Faulkner described its sound as “spectacular,” with several equalizer options available for helping fine-tune the sound to your liking. Read our review.

Ikea’s Poäng chair balances affordability and comfort, with wide, supportive armrests that are just the right height for holding a controller. It comes in a variety of frame materials and cushion colors, too, making it easy to match your other furniture or console. This is my personal chair for console gaming, and I recently added a matching ottoman to the set for maximum relaxation.
